ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e3675	We're now on the 1st. Question No 114, by Mr Preiner. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e3696	Mr. Federal Minister! The last winter was very warm, so the bee peoples were very much added. The Beekeepers' Federation speaks of up to 70 percent loss of bee peoples throughout Austria. Why has the research project “Future Biene”, which is carried out by the TU Graz and the AGES, been set for results presentations in 2017, although the Austrian Plant Protection Act restricts the ban on the use of three neonicotinoids by 2016? We in the National Council therefore have the duty to find 2016 an extension of the ban on the use of the three neonicotinoides. The request submitted in writing, 114/M, has the following wording: [[Quoted print version removed]]
ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e3728	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e3743	Madam President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! With the umbrella association of the Austrian beekeepers and the federal states, we have commissioned the University of Graz to carry out the research project “Future Biene”, the project management lies with Univ.-Prof. Crailsheim. This is a serious, economic research project. Therefore, the duration was determined in such a way that well-founded results can be ensured, and that is exactly the term until 2017. The aim is to promote research in bee health in Austria and to understand all influences on bee people and bee losses. Thus, the causes are to be identified and demonstrated on a scientific basis away from prejudices. The project makes an important contribution to the development of measures to reduce losses and improve the health status of bee peoples in the future. We have eight modules: from winter mortality, cause research into bee people and bee losses, individual damage tolerance, carrier plant diversity, organization and international contacts. But of course there will also be interim reports. These are the basis for an appropriate assessment and analysis of the question of the extension of the moratorium for neonicotinoides.

In agriculture, it is about avoiding methane's emissions, as you have rightly said, and above all nitrogen monoxide from animal husbandry and from the use of economic fertilizers and mineral fertilizers, as well as carbon dioxide from machine management. The implementation already takes place in 2015, mainly within the framework of the current rural development programme 2014 to 2020, which I have already discussed, and the Special Directives adopted. In this way, the rural development programme is also an extremely efficient climate protection programme. The financing is carried out jointly by the Federal Government, the Länder and the European Union. These are a bundle of approaches, such as reducing support and awareness-raising measures for agricultural holdings. the development, demonstration and information measures to promote land premiums, such as the agricultural environment programme or organic farming, to support projects in the field of investment, cooperation, innovation partnership, as well as the promotion of climate-related agricultural research projects.

Austria has, and this shows this result, an exemplary wastewater treatment and waste management system. But of course there is also a need for action. That's why we've had a ten-point programme developed, which was held on 12 December. ==References====External links== It contains a bundle of measures at national and EU level that can contribute to the elimination of plastic and microplastic pollution in waters. We need uniform measurement methods and standards across the EU in order to achieve a comparable data situation. The methods developed by the UBA for this special exam are unique, since we are certainly pioneers. Other important measures include, for example, the implementation of the EU Plastics Sackerl Directive, the microplastics exit from cosmetics and detergents, awareness-building measures up to the “Zero Pellets Loss” pact that we have adopted with the plastics industry. A motion for a resolution was also adopted in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection. In addition, on the initiative of my department, on 11 and 12 November, the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to turnover taxes. A microplastics conference was held in Together with representatives of industry, the network of European environmental agencies, the European Commission, with NGOs and other Member States, in particular the Netherlands, we have discussed concrete steps to eliminate microplastic pollution.

I will present a proposal for a change in the forest law in this context. Thank you.

But whether it is actually being built is not yet decided. This depends very much on the question of support and aid for this power plant, and at present all the relevant decisions have been postponed. However, we remain vigilant, and it is remarkable that our announcement to complain against the subsidisation of Hankley Point C will probably lead to a delay even now in the Czech Republic and possibly to the end of the expansion process. There is no legal means of preventing nuclear power plants, provided certain minimum criteria are met. The EIA procedure has been formally correct. On your question: I am of course in constant contact with my Czech Environment Minister-College Brabec. We meet with the Environment Councils on a regular basis and we also have bilateral discussions, especially on nuclear safety.

In order to link what is necessary in the production of nuclear power plants up to those processing and deposition measures, which, of course, also employ humans enormously afterwards and, for decades, have their effects, or in fact, for centuries. Unfortunately, we had to experience nuclear energy disasters, and we would like to point out that we can also close wide alliances beyond our national borders in order to prevent such things in the future. The nuclear accident of Fukushima occurred a few years ago, and Chernobyl's catastrophe is now a few years behind. In this context, however, it is unfortunately always noted that, when such disasters occur, there is great horror at the moment. Policymakers in this world have demonstrated their participation and have also announced their rethinking. And what do you notice? – That often a short-term memory is used. After a few weeks or months, when the headlines are no longer controlled by this catastrophe, suddenly the old pride begins to fall again. You may then feel: This is not so tragic, and modern technology will save us from one or another in the future. But that's a tricky conclusion! If we really light up these two disasters, Chernobyl 1987 and Fukushima in Japan four years ago, then we need to know that hundreds of thousands of people have been directly damaged, killed, seriously wounded, they have suffered sustainable health damage and have also been passed on to the next generations, that entire land has been depopulated, the animal and plant world has been permanently damaged, in fact a true human disaster and tragedy has occurred. We should take this opportunity, ladies and gentlemen, to continue to act against nuclear power stations in a bundled manner, as the Austrian Parliament has already documented several times. We need to know something about this: On the European continent too, one was horrified and promised rethinking in many countries. In the meantime, however, measures have been taken which, in turn, scare us because nuclear power plants are being rebuilt and there are plans to further expand nuclear power. So in the UK, for example, it happens with Hinkley Point C: In this context, Austria has taken a very basic step by bringing an action. This has, of course, brought great international attention. But as Parliament and the Austrian Federal Government, we stand to face this abjection, and I think we must carry it through with all due consequence, we and our children are really guilty, because it cannot be the answer today to the questions and problems of energy policy only with nuclear policy. We are currently providing enormous alternative sources of energy, whose technology and use are progressing further. With this in mind, both in Austria and on the European continent, we can put in place huge measures and really show visions to secure energy supply in a broad way alternative and sustainable manner. In fact, nuclear energy seems to be only environmentally friendly at the moment. Opponents can cite many aspects of why this is not so. It only takes a single accident to happen, and all that has been spent has been destroyed for centuries. Secondly, it is also about the final storage. As we know, our neighbouring countries have also already put in place plans to expand end-to-end warehouses in the immediate vicinity of Austria. On the other hand, we also have to act massively! Dear Ladies and gentlemen! In the future, EURATOM, a merger of the European States in order to address precisely these issues, must also be ready to reorientate. In the future, EURATOM must be geared towards nuclear research being concerned with the safety aspects, not to advance development and achieve sustainable development of nuclear installations. There must be a rethinking in order to focus the security aspects. Therefore, our focus will also be on participation in EURATOM on this issue. [[Abg. Pirklhuber: We need money for renewable energy!]] Ladies and gentlemen! Austria has taken advantage of its opportunities. Austria has a few decades ago No, in terms of nuclear policy, and therefore we must now use our powers to infect others and bring them closer to the current sources of sustainable energy. A mix of solar energy, wind and hydropower and renewable raw materials is available to us in this sense, and we will drive this mix forward, and we will also convince other people, far beyond our borders. [[Abg. Pirklhuber: For this we need money!]] Mr. Federal Minister, thank you very much for your efforts and your commitment to all these issues in order to act against nuclear power internationally, but also thank you for your commitment to drive the sustainable energy economy in Austria! The Hinkley Point C nuclear power plant is supported by €34.3 billion as a result of the European Commission's decision, as much as the construction costs will be. For two water pressure reactors with 1,600 megawatts each, the power installed per kilowatt corresponds to a euro amount of almost € 11 000. According to British analysts, the nuclear power plant will be the most expensive nuclear power station in the world. Figures will be for the UK taxpayers with a State guaranteed purchase price of 12.6 Eurocents per kilowatt hour. Today, the market price is 3.2 Eurocent. We see that the four-fold price is necessary for the nuclear power plant to be operated. At present, about 9.27 Eurocents are paid for wind energy and for hydropower, depending on the quantity, between 8 and 10.4 Eurocents. With today's decision, we want to support Mr. Federal Minister, but also the Federal Government with our Federal Chancellor, so that they can take legal action against this decision. We support the government and ask for your help.

Even in our country, in Austria, this problem is taking its course, here it is about the entries in our waters to the minimum extent, which then also add to enormous numbers and create loads for our waters. We're talking about microparticles in the real sense. These particles are usually less than five millimeters, which are entered in the waters by various forms of source. On the one hand, these particles, which are often used in cosmetics, are used in cleaning agents, for example, to increase their cleaning performance, and do not have a direct negative impact on the environment, but which, in the future, also enters the wastewater. That's the one cause. The second cause is precisely the abrasions caused by the laundry, which unfortunately are also caused by Letisring, i.e. garbage, plastic articles that are unfortunately thrown away uncontrolled in our landscape. And we have to counter this, because we know that the vast majority – that is, more than 90 percent – is done by so-called diffuse entries: discarded plastic, plastic that enters the sewer, and much more. This, of course, also results in a considerable amount of water that is being flooded back into the large waters, into the rivers or even into the sea, which starts to deposition or is also absorbed by the animal world and thus returns to the food chain, which directly affects people and does not prevent health effects. In Austria, the Federal Minister also responded when the first figures became known and the first investigations showed up. Of course, he has also been associated with the industry, has implemented a catalogue of measures on a voluntary basis, because our aim is not to set limits, but in reality to move these entries in the direction of zero. Therefore, efforts to work on different levels in this way, in cooperation with producers, but also those who use these resources, and above all, it is also about creating awareness that plastic is not simply uncontrolled and unmotivated in the landscape. We have already tabled this request and discussed it in the Committee, but because this issue does not stop outside Austria's borders, because the Danube flows not only through Austria but also through other countries, this phenomenon is one of the whole of Europe that we want to devote ourselves to. That is why, and I am now on behalf of Mr Brunner, we want to give a mandate from the House, the Parliament of the Federal Government and, in particular, the Federal Minister. ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e8448	Mr President. High house! Let me also comment on this very essential issue of microplastic pollution. It is, I believe, unanimous consensus in the hall that plastic and microplastics do not belong to our environment and not to our waters at all. Rivers and seas do not stop at national borders, so measures against microplastic pollution are also necessary at European, international and global level. Exploration – and this has been rightly addressed by Mrs Weigerstorfer – of the possible ecological effects of microplastic pollution in water and, unfortunately, especially in the world seas is only beginning to occur. There are already similar research initiatives, and, remarkably, Austria is one of the pioneers in the field of limnology. The development of a plastic measuring barometer suitable for routine monitoring for microplastics is methodologically very complex. This shows all the relevant work of the Federal Environment Agency. We have therefore expressed our full support for a Europe-wide approach. At the conference against pollution from plastic and microplastics, initiated by us, which took place on 11 and 12. In May, in Brussels, representatives of the Dutch Environment Ministry, participants from partner organisations and non-governmental organisations have for the first time worked together on this subject. Commissioner Vella has also participated. We will maintain the pressure for a European initiative on the part of the European Commission, both by the Environment Council and, as I hope, by the European Parliament. When the topic of plastic came up in the Danube last spring, I immediately acted and commissioned a special investigation along the Danube. The Federal Environment Agency has developed a special measurement method for this purpose. We have discussed the results of this special study in very detailed terms in the Committee on the Environment, Public Health and Consumer Protection and have discussed it very constructively. The result shows – and this is positive – that in Austria we have a very exemplary system of wastewater treatment and waste management. One of the possible sources of microplastic pollution is primary microplastics, which are the microplastic balls, which, as already mentioned, are deliberately attached by industry to cosmetics, for example toothpaste. I voted in favour of an EU-wide ban on microplastics in cosmetics at the Environmental Ministers Council in December 2014. And I am very happy about this – this is a concrete outcome of the Microplastics Conference – that the industry involved here has expressed itself in the run-up to the Microplastics Conference that there will be a voluntary exit from microplastics by the end of 2015. In this way, we can already report a very favourable positive decision. [[Abg. Brunner:... if ever everything is voluntary!]] A second source of microplastics – which you, Mrs Brunner, have also rightly addressed – is the so-called secondary microplastics, such as the abrasion of textiles while washing. In this area, the starting point lies with the manufacturers of washing machines. There are already appropriate research initiatives, and in a relatively short time it is quite possible to eliminate this abrasion as a source of microplastics. Another and probably most of the microplastics, which is also secondary microplastics, is created by larger plastic objects, so to speak, from macroplastics, which mainly enters the environment through Literring, where it becomes microplastics through UV light and friction and then pollutes our waters. There is therefore a need for action, which is why I have adopted my Ten Points Programme for the Quality of Our Flowwaters, which sets up both at EU level and at national level and, as a very essential measure, involves the development of a uniform measurement methodology at European level. For the future, I consider the implementation of the EU Plastics Sackerl Directive and a comprehensive microplastic exit in cosmetics to be particularly important. In the elimination of the sources, I also put quite consciously on voluntaryness, on the responsibility of businesses and on our citizens' own responsibility. Especially in the context of Letisring, it is important to put in place many awareness-raising measures. Self-responsibilities and non-compulsory measures make us successful in this area, because our consumers, our citizens, are ready to make an active contribution here. This is particularly important for an EU-wide approach and research activity with accredited measurement methods, so that one can really seek a common clarification of open questions – for example, how microplastics behave in waters. Austria has a pioneering role in many areas of the environment, and I would like to start by saying: Perhaps Austria could also be involved in the JPI – Joint Programming Initiative. This is an EU-wide research platform dealing, among other things, with microplastics in marine ecosystems. Even if Austria is an inland land, the waste is also created here and enters the seas. I am convinced that only a common approach at European level, involving all stakeholders, is a goal. In this way, we can obtain well-founded, comparable data, from which we can then derive measures. This will lead – the Federal Minister has already mentioned it – to progress in avoiding plastic and microplastics, coupled with increased awareness among producers and consumers. For these reasons, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to ask you to accept this motion for a resolution. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e8654	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! Microplastics has lost nothing in the environment. You can't stress that often enough. Everything must therefore be done to reduce the amounts of microplastics entering the environment and especially the waters as quickly as possible. As has already been said, microplastics are called smallest parts of plastics, which can be found today in many products of daily use and whose residues reach the rivers and seas via the waste water. The United Nations Environment Programme assumes that up to 18,000 plastic or plastic parts are swimming on each square kilometer of ocean. It will take centuries for these plastics to be crushed and mined by physical, chemical and biological processes. The Environment Committee presented a study by the Federal Environment Agency on plastic in the Danube. Thus, the transport of total plastics in the Danube is up to 145 kilograms per day. 90 percent of these come from surface drainage, discarded utility goods from construction sites or from improper use of products. However, it is not only about the Danube and other regional waters, but also about the need to work across countries to improve the situation. Microplastics in the Danube do not stop at our borders. Therefore, an exchange and comparison of information and measures within the EU is extremely important so that the growth of microplastics in the waters does not continue as before. This is the one thing: to raise awareness at European level and to work actively towards something going on. But the knowledge of the matter alone is not enough. It must also be applied properly and the necessary steps must also be taken. This is required in our request, and I am confident that these demands will also be implemented accordingly. But the other side – and this is a special concern to me – concerns each one of us. It is up to all of us to pay attention to our own plastic waste in everyday life and to counter it accordingly. This is not about jute instead of plastic, but about simply becoming aware of his own responsibility. Microplastics is today contained in many daily uses, including washing and cleaning products, care products, cosmetics, shower gels, liquid soap et cetera. The list is long, which is why it is also understandable why our waters are so burdened. A focus of the proposal is on the development of concrete initiatives to eliminate microplastic pollution in waters and seas, in particular to eliminate pollution sources, because what is not produced at all does not have to be removed effortlessly. Let us, therefore, agree together with our proposal for an increased commitment at European level and for a viable environment! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e8944	Mr President. Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In agriculture, our production base, our soil, plays a special, actually existential role. Humus is that little layer that decides how fruitful our soils are. The Humus layer decides whether all our efforts, all of our work will be crowned with success – say: with a good harvest. It is true that the humus layer around the world is not really handled responsibly. On the contrary: In many regions of the world, robbery is being built on our soils and thus on our future. In addition, as a wonderful and important accompanying appearance, our soil is also an immense storage for CO2. In our farmlands, 4.6 times as much CO2 is stored as we issued in 2007. If we managed to increase the humus share by only 1 percent, this would mean an additional annual CO2 binding of 3.2 million tons. However, one thing has to be said clearly: We can improve CO2 balance through this effort, but we cannot save the climate. This requires much more efforts to be made together. [[Complementary of the Abg. Aubauer.]] An essential point in this context, which cannot be mentioned, is the incredible consumption of agricultural land. In Austria, 12 hectares of agricultural land are installed daily or otherwise sealed. We cannot make up for this loss with all the efforts of the humus building. We will have to devote more and more to this issue. [[New accompaniment of the abg. Aubauer.]] Our local agricultural environment programme includes a variety of measures that help to protect erosion and enrich humus in our agricultural soils. The aim is to ensure the proportion of the humus and maintain the profitability of the agricultural soils. In addition to the maintenance of permanent pastures by appropriate land promotions, soil erosion and humus degradation in arable soils are affected in particular by the installation of grounds, the use of various fruit consequences, as well as by reduced soil processing and the preservation of landscape elements. The main measures in this context in the ÖPUL are: organic farming, greening of arable land or intermediate crops, erosion protection, greening of arable land in the system Immergrün, mulch and direct seed and, of course, the environmentally-friendly and biodiversity-promoting management. All these measures aim at sustainable conservation and also to build up the humus layer in order to maintain the yield capacity of the soils or to increase CO2 storage capacity. All these measures are used intensively by farmers in Austria. Nearly 90 percent of all farmers participate in this voluntary programme. So you see, the reality has overtaken the intention of this motion for a resolution. We continue to rely on voluntary participation in the highly successful environmental programme in the future, because we know that farmers in this country are aware of the importance of the humus layer for our farms, but also of the importance for all of us. The intention of this request is quite correct. But we have been working on it for decades and reject the pressure associated with this request.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e9094	Mr Feichtinger came next. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e9116	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! Before entering the real topic, perhaps a personal statement. Mr Federal Minister, if you would like to work for a nuclear-free Europe in the future: My – and I think our – support is yours! I'm changing the subject and coming to TOP 6. The present proposal by the Stronach team on the subject of lead in drinking water deals with a problem which was the subject of a decision of the Supreme Court many years ago. In principle, I would like to say here that contaminations like those with lead in drinking water have not lost anything. In this respect, the aim of the request by the colleague, Mr Refuserstorfer, is a correct one. If lead pipes inside the house are causal for a drinking water contamination, the landlord shall, in principle, make a fixation obligation, regardless of whether they are pipes in the rental property itself or pipes in the general parts of the house. In its 2004 decision, the OGH discussed whether the landlord's obligation to maintain water contaminated by lead was met and stated in a first examination that lead pipes would then, if they did not affect the substance of the house in themselves and did not pose a risk to the house itself, not be subject to the landlord's maintenance obligation. At the time, he noted that if a minor effort, such as a water advance of one minute, could be eliminated from the supply of drinking water, there would be no damage to the building that would be subject to the landlord’s maintenance obligation. After this decision, the tenant is told to let the faucet run for a minute to draw drinking water. After this minute, the lead load was usually in the range of 10 to 20 micrograms per litre, which was below the then valid limit of 25 micrograms per litre. On the grounds of the request of the Stronach team, it is now stated that, after the limit value has been reduced to 10 micrograms per litre of tenants, the water can be verified on a fee basis and in the event of a proven border crossing, the owner can complain, but lengthy court proceedings and cost-intensive opinions can be expected if one wants to ensure the supply of lead-free drinking water. The description of the proper legal path in this case, however, seems to us to be the correct way to finally clarify the question in the room in the sense of legal certainty for landlords and tenants. We have therefore decided to reject the motion for a resolution which, in the light of the now lowered limit values, will have to take into account the interests already mentioned above in Austria's case law.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e9414	Mr President. High house! Let me also briefly comment on these three agenda items. First of all, on the item on the agenda concerning the building of humus, on the request of Mr Hofer. Well, it is true that the conservation or development of soils is important in a number of ways towards optimal humus content. Humus is not only an important carbon memory, and thus a CO2 sink, but also an important prerequisite for the fertility and profitability of our soils. This has already been correctly noted in previous debates. That is why, in our rural development programme, we have given these measures, the humus-building and carbon-enriching measures, a central priority, because the humus building is also a climate protection measure and, of course, is very important for the soil fertility of the arable soils, but also for the green soils. The positive impact of the measures is clearly demonstrated by the evaluations of the programmes, the various measures we have carried out in the rural development programmes over the last 15 years. Measures on organic farming, greening in arable farming, field feed and the use of organic fertilisers in combination with fruit consequences, but also, for example, the planting of the pastures as a whole, are humus-forming. You have to find out. [[Abg. Moser: Less sealing and less sealing!]] In principle, the implementation of the humus building measures over the past 15 years has significantly contributed to this positive development of humus building. We have therefore deliberately strengthened the measures in the rural development programme for climate protection and soil fertility. As far as Mr Pock's request on the question of tax relief for coal electricity is concerned, I, and I believe everyone here, agree with the assessment that a modern, sustainable, renewable energy system for coal generation should not actually be in place in the long term. From the point of view, it is also positive – we have discussed this in the Committee – that the Austrian power plant operators are at least willing to provide for or phase out the decommissioning of existing coal power plants, and that new sites are no longer under discussion. On the other hand, it is unfortunate that at European level, unfortunately, electricity generation has not been able to reduce coal production through EU emissions trading. Unfortunately, in Germany it is currently en vogue to put cheap coal back into use. It must be resisted because, from my point of view, this is not a sustainable and not a sustainable energy system. As for the question of the tax exemption mentioned above, I can refer to the Coal Tax Act and address the competence of the Federal Minister for Finance. You know the subject of double taxation – the electricity tax on the one hand and the tax exemption for coal on the other. From an environmental point of view, I think everyone agrees that this is a counterproductive matter. In this context, I would also like to refer once again to the very positive energy balance in 2014, which shows a marked decline in fossil energy sources, i.e. coal falling by 18 percent, coke minus 26 percent, fuel oil minus 29 percent. [[Abg. Brunner: This is a year!]] [[Abg. Brunner: This is a year!]] [[Abg. Pirklhuber: Milder Winter!]] You see, here is the approach for renewable energy sources. On the request for “lead in drinking water” by Mrs Weigerstorfer, I can predict that this issue does not fall within my sphere of competence, which we have already discussed in the Committee, but rather, in connection with the drinking water regulation, in the case of the Federal Minister for Health. Compliance with the limit of ten micrograms per litre is undoubtedly necessary in order to protect particularly sensitive groups of people – such as pregnant women, unborn, infants and infants – as you have rightly addressed. The sources of lead in drinking water are usually lead pipes of house connections, house installations and fittings. The water supplier is still responsible for these connection lines within the meaning of the Food Safety and Consumer Protection Act. For the Federal Government, there is currently no legal possibility to arrange or promote measures for the exchange of lead pipes and house installations. In Vienna, at least since 2007, it has succeeded in replacing all lead pipes in house connection lines. In my area of responsibility, we pay particular attention to the fact that 40 years old pipelines are now being exchanged. This is also the case here in the House by extending the grant framework in the context of the settlement water management. This is also a positive contribution in this context. – Thank you very much for the attention.

It is gratifying that as the declared corporate objective of the association, the share of renewable energy is also determined to be increased from currently 95 percent to 100 percent. Austria has a role to play in this regard, compared with the 70 million tax relief mentioned in the application, the situation in other countries is much more dramatic. For example, in Germany, an average of 2.7 billion have been invested in coal mining in recent years. ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e9694	As a provisional last item, I should like to speak on this item on the agenda, Mr Schmid. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e9716	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The topic of drinking water in lead pipelines: The subject of drinking water via lead pipelines should be considered from a medical and technical point of view. Incontrovertible is that drinking water pipes from lead can cause serious health damage. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] The following are to be addressed: Brain damage, cramps up to coma as well as cancer. The present application addresses the stock of drinking water pipes in the objects of the founding period. Until 1914 the drinking water pipes were trained exclusively from lead, from 1914 to 1945 about 3 percent of new buildings were equipped with lead lines, and since 1983 lead pipes for drinking water have generally been no longer installed. The lead content in drinking water was set at 25 micrograms per litre for Austria and the EU in 2003 with a transitional period of up to 2013 and then lowered to 10 micrograms per litre. With the Austrian Drinking Water Regulation, in the German Law 304 of 2001, it is stated that from 1 January 2001 onwards. The limit for lead in drinking water is reduced to a maximum of 0.01 milligrams per litre on December 2013. From a technical point of view, it should be noted that drinking water supply is in the area of the municipalities, the responsibility of the legislator and the supplier ends at the water clock. Supply lines in the property itself are exclusively within the responsibility of the owner or of house management under the conditions of the aforementioned legal requirements. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Muttons.]] It should be noted that the conversion of the drinking water supply of lead pipelines is to be considered completed in accordance with the state of the art. The life of lead pipelines is limited, so it can be assumed that drinking water pipes from the years before 1914, which would therefore be over 100 years old, are no longer in use. It is appropriate to implement the applicable laws and the drinking water regulation accordingly and, where appropriate, to review their implementation. The request is therefore to be rejected from our point of view. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e10482	Mr President. High house! Let me also briefly comment on the rescue of the Uhudler. I can also get out here: I'm a big fan of the Uhudler myself. I was also involved when we were able to establish the guarantee in 1994 in the accession negotiations with this transitional regime. I went to the Committee on Agriculture on 6 June. May of this year I promise that I will accept this matter in order to secure the Uhudler permanently beyond the 2030 deadline. I'm very serious, too. The Uhudler has – already mentioned in all the debates – tradition and modernity. It stands for the identity of the region of the South Burgenland and is a very strong identification feature. One can say that he is something like a cult drink in the South of Burgenland today. I would almost say that the Uhudler is in the South of Burgenland something like the Krautinger in the Wildschönau: Everyone's identified with it. But it is also true that income opportunities and value added are created in the region, and it is therefore necessary to ensure this regional speciality on a permanent basis. In EU law – you know – the current EU Regulation 1308/2013 on the Market Organisation for Agricultural Products provides that only those cellar grapes belonging to or derived from Vitis vinifera may be classified, and this is not true of the Uhudler. He is one of the direct carrier varieties that, as has already been said, came to Austria 150 years ago in order to counteract the severe reblue attack, and which at that time also prevailed as resistant varieties. The EU Regulation has duly settled down in the wine-growing regulations of the Federal States of Burgenland and Lower Austria, and the transitional period negotiated in the Accession Treaty is foreseen by 2030. The implementation of wine law is exclusively in national competence, which means the authorisation of grape varieties, the legal assessment and the establishment of legal effects. In the case of infringements, the state regulations are the responsibility of the federal states. Now, on the basis of a proposal from the Hamburg Agricultural Council of Liegenfeld, we have chosen the starting point that, on the basis of a reform of the Wine Law, we are creating a transfer of the Uhudler from the wine sector to the fruit wine sector. I am going to present you with a government paper on this, so let me send it to the House. This enables us to create a permanent and above all safe national solution for the Uhudler producers and thus the basis for the Uhudler friends to enjoy this cult drink beyond 2030. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] This proposal comes from the region itself. I also had the opportunity yesterday to discuss this arrangement with the representatives of the producers, the chairman and the vice-president of the association. We will therefore implement this proposal on the basis of a reform of the wine law, which will soon be sent to the House. Further, it should also be useful - I think the association will act accordingly - to obtain, in addition to this security, recognition as a regional designation of origin within the meaning of the relevant EU Regulation. This can be ensured, Mr Doppler: Uhudler will remain Uhudler in the future. [[Abg. Doppler: Very good!]] And free to Kennedy when it comes to the Uhudler: I'm a Southburger! Thank you.

Glyphosate is a herbicide, which is produced in huge quantities, especially in the conventional agricultural sector, but is also used in many households. A study by the World Health Organization considers glyphosate to be a cancer-stimulating fact that cannot be simply erased from the table. Glyphosate is also strongly suspected of being at least co-responsible for bee dying. The global death of amphibians also seems to be due to the massive use of insecticide. There is also a study that proves that there are extremely worrying concentrations in the waters adjacent to herbicide, where herbicide is used. All this – especially the carcinogenic effect – has already caused the Migros Group in Switzerland to remove glyphosate-containing herbicide completely from the range. Other countries have already taken it from the range. What people in our country do not understand is the attitude of the EIA and the Minister for Agriculture on this issue. The damage to the population is also averted. So I stand for the ban on glyphosate – for the people, not for Roundup or Monsanto. Quite clearly, I am in favour of suspending the sale of glyphosate. This means is used not only by large farms but also by ASFINAG or the ÖBB in large quantities, and it is also sold, as has been said, in retail trade. I am constantly being addressed by concerned people in Austria, especially in Salzburg, why glyphosate is still being used. The bio-agricultural sector in particular wants a comprehensive ban on glycosate. Organic farms do not see why glyphosate can be entered in their fields by wind, weather, water. What many farmers already know, the final customers in the gardens, in the households, do not know. So I also advocate more information on this. At least people should know that they are carrying out things that are not completely inconceivable. For example, Greenpeace Austria has identified residues in our food in connection with the Umweltbundesamt – of course in small concentrations, as you will now think. It is true, however, that carcinogenic substances can be found in our food. I wonder why the ÖVP is still saying no to this ban. Glyphosate, carcinogenic substances in our food, I'm sure there's no such thing as that.

Then the asylum seekers, the authorised asylum seekers, will be taken to private quarters in the individual Länder, namely those federal states that have not yet fulfilled the 100 percent quota. On question 21: Of a total of 2,635 unaccompanied minors, 2,338 asylum proceedings are pending at the BFA or at the Federal Administrative Court. On the question 22: On the 21st day of the year. In May 2015, the Federal Office of Care in East Traiskirchen, in Reichenau an der Rax, in Styria, in Steinhaus am Semmering, in the Magdeburg-Kaserne in Klosterneuburg, and in the districts of the federal states, unaccompanied minors were accommodated. On questions 23-25: In 2014, 5 993 consultation procedures with regard to transfers from Austria were conducted to Dublin-Out procedures and 1,327 transfers from Austria were carried out in accordance with “Dublin-Out Procedures”. In 2014, 2 355 consultation procedures with regard to transfers to Austria were conducted under “Dublin-In-Procedures”. 790 transfers were made to Austria. However, the figures should still be considered provisional due to possible recasting and data adjustment. On the question 26: No Dublin transfers are currently being made to Greece. On the question 27: Absolute priority must be given to the full implementation of the legal acts of the European asylum system with regard to the standards for the accommodation and management of asylum procedures. Only by ensuring that asylum seekers are treated equally across Europe and reducing incentives for secondary migration. In addition, the Dublin system remains one of the cornerstones of the European asylum system, and further efficiency needs to be improved. This can be achieved by ensuring that all Member States lose their fingerprints without delay in the case of newly arrived irregular migrants, in order to facilitate identification on a journey, as provided for in the Eurodac Regulation. In addition, national measures must be adopted. On the question 28: The executives use the instrument of the so-called compensation measures, in short AGM. The principle task of the AGM forces is to ensure police controls in border regions and on the main transit roads. The level of staff currently amounts to more than 1 300 executive staff in the federal states at the disposal of the National Police Directorates. In addition, the operational centre is set up at the BMI, which supports around 65 staff in priority actions. In 2014, around 155 tugs were arrested by the specially trained orga­nization unit and 1,364 towed or illegally stopped. On question 29: Joint measures to combat illegal migration will be discussed regularly during expert discussions and high-level appointments between Austria and Italy. As an example, the bi- and trilateral stripes of Germany, Austria and Italy can be led on the road and on the train. Due to the current migration pressure, joint lines with Italy between Udine and Villach were also established. Question 30: This cooperation takes place at several levels. Firstly, within the European Union and neighbouring countries. This is reflected in the ministerial meetings like the one recently held. Visit of the French Minister of Interior in Vienna or in discussions at expert level with Italian representatives on asylum and migration. Secondly, there is intensive cooperation with transit states, especially the Western Balkans. But the main focus is on cooperation with third countries. The Western Balkans were invited to this year's Forum-Salzburg Ministerial Conference in Austria to discuss the existing problems together and to consolidate cooperation. Europol, Frontex, UNHCR and EASO also participated in the conference. Thirdly, the internal establishment works closely with local authorities through, inter alia, Austrian liaison officers in third countries or countries of origin. There is no doubt that the police attachés have a key role to play in the direct transmission of information on illegal migration and the current situation on the ground to the Austrian authorities. On question 31: An efficient asylum system can only work if it is clearly differentiated between genuine refugees of war and those who have left their homes for economic reasons only, just as we do in the law. For this purpose, various measures are provided for under the Foreign Rights Change Act, such as the management of accelerated procedures for persons from safe countries of origin or the restriction of basic services in the case of obviously unfounded asylum applications. In order to prevent the illegal immigration of economic refugees across Europe, economic refugees are already to be identified in third countries and distinguished from vulnerable refugees. This is the objective of the Austrian “Save Lives” initiative, namely the initial examination of the need for protection by UNHCR directly in the countries of origin or transit. As a result, vulnerable refugees can be offered a safe way into the EU while combating illegal migration more effectively. We need to destroy the business model of the tractors that deliberately put people in distress. Therefore, our proposal is to first bring Gerettete to UNHC sites at Seenot Gerettete, where an initial examination and selection is carried out by UNHCR, and then to distribute it with a fixed key across Europe. In the medium and long term, however, the sustainable fight against refugee issues is necessary through close cooperation between all policy areas, from development cooperation to trade to external and internal policy. On question 32: On the part of the BMI, it was ordered to carry out the most timely and complete pre-examination of passengers from risk destinations on arriving flights and to pay particular attention to passengers corresponding to certain risk indicators in the context of the actual border control. Checks in the framework of compensation measures are more targeted in view of the current, continuously re-evaluated situation than the stationary border controls which are calculated by the tractors. Control is carried out in those places and places in Austria where our police analysis results are more likely to result in seizures. This ensures maximum flexibility. In addition to this, targeted enhanced controls in the border areas, including along the green border, are carried out on the basis of appropriate analyses. On question 33: The Federal Ministry of the Interiors has entered into a lease with a private person directly concerning the Bundessorgungsstelle Salzburg. On question 34: Salzburg is located in the district of Salzburg-Stadt. On question 35: On the 21st day of the year. In May 2015, 34 persons were accommodated in the gymnasium of the Landespolizeidirektion Salzburg, 38 in the gymnasium of the Landes police department Burgenland and 34 in the gymnasium of the Oberösterreich. On question 36: On the 21st day of the year. On May 18, 2015, 18 persons were accommodated in tents on the grounds of the West Federal Service Office in Thalham, 61 persons in tents on the territory of the Oberösterreich State Police Directorate and 50 persons in tents on the site of the Salzburg Land Police Directorate. On the question 37: No other sites for tents are currently planned; I say immediately: depending of course also on the next days. I'd rather destroy them than add new ones. On the question 38: The Federal Ministry of the Interior is seeking to comply with the agreements made, taking into account the current exceptional situation. Question 39: For the execution of the Schubhaft, around 410 people were stopped in Vordenberg since the opening until April 2015. Due to the current migration pressure and the specified purpose of the Anhaltezentrum Fürderenberg, existing resources are currently being used there for the first asylum survey and for recognition. On question 40: On the side of the Federal Ministry of the Interior, no contracts were concluded with NGOs on the care of helpless and vulnerable foreigners in the Federal Services. The care is provided exclusively by a private operator in the federal care facilities. Question 41: The answer to this question does not fall within the scope of the interior. On question 42: The implementation of the transparency database is not within the scope of internal competence, but within the financial sphere. On question 43: In 2014 the Federal Ministry of the Interior of the Federal Republic of Germany attacked 800 €156.88 in the costs of legal advice. On question 44: The total cost of 2014 is not yet available due to current settlements. These are the detailed questions I hope have answered to your satisfaction. I am particularly grateful to our experts of the House and also to the Federal Office for Foreign Affairs and Asylum for the rapid preparation of the answer to these questions: Thanks to the experts!

It is such a kind of martial affair that may also serve to meet certain security needs of the population. ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e20004	Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mr President. 34 out of 183 people have just spoken in favour of introducing temporary border controls. As the agenda wants, the answer for the 34 is already the presentation of these two state agreements. Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! With these two state agreements, we ensure that cross-border crime can now be truly pursued by a borderless police and that these police are now able to pursue any crime that goes beyond the border accordingly. I can therefore only thank all officials from the Ministry of the Interior and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs who negotiated this agreement with Slovakia and Italy. I should also like to thank all of you, because it is a treaty that is now being agreed by all. In other words, we have not only a borderless police, we also have a party-border cooperation in the field of security. This is important for the safety of Austrians. These two treaties ensure that criminals who are trying to flee the border can no longer escape alone because of the state border, but that the Austrian police can rush after them and continue to observe them when they cross the state border. In addition, the Treaties allow for enhanced cooperation in the protection of witnesses and in the protection of victims. With these contracts, a state border between Slovakia and Austria is never to the advantage of criminals, but only more of the police. This will ensure security and cooperation. I would like to thank you once again, and I would like to ask for further cooperation in the field of security for the Austrians.

Gerstl, with all the ladies and gentlemen – especially those of the police – who ensure that we have such a good security standard in Austria. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Cooperation with our immediate neighbours in particular plays a key role in day-to-day police. Refugee criminals, international criminals cartels or corruption are just making Don't stop at our state borders. Without cooperation with our immediate European neighbours, modern police work, a high security standard keyword in Austria, would be impossible at all levels. With our two neighbours Slovakia and Italy, Austria has been combining successful police cooperation for many years. The corresponding agreement with Slovakia dates back to 2004 and has already included joint police strips in recent years. These are now being extended, and the international anti-corruption system on the European arrest warrant is now being incorporated into treaties. In addition, both countries will support themselves in the transport system if necessary – and this is not irrelevant either. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The agreement with Italy dates back to 1997. The new agreement repeals the existing restrictions on cross-border follow-up, as stated in the legislative text, to cross-border persecution. Just to remember: While previously volatile suspects could be persecuted only about 20 kilometres on motorways and 10 kilometres on national roads in the respective territory of the country, there are no longer any time and space restrictions in the future. The aftertaste – the persecution – on the airway is now permitted. Ladies and gentlemen, mutual support in the area of illegal immigration is also provided for. Where necessary, joint centres may be set up to facilitate the exchange of information and to assist the competent authorities. In our networked global world, not only crime, but also police work has long since overcome state borders. The modernisation of the police cooperation agreements with Slovakia and Italy takes account of this fact. We will all benefit from the joint work of colleagues on both sides of the borders in the future. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2015-05-21-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00075_d8e21041	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! It has already been said a lot about the Federal Museum Act, so I would like to refer to the Convention to be ratified in my contribution to the debate. This is about measures to protect cultural property against its illegal exports and to combat illegal trade in cultural goods. Such measures are, of course, to be welcomed. The acceptance of such measures is not least reflected in the fact that it is quite natural for public and private museums as well as collectors today to examine the provenance of the objects in their acquisitions and to ensure the legality of their origin. For the ratification of this Convention, it is also said that, except Liechtenstein, this already applies to all our neighbouring countries. The explanations point out that Austria is therefore in an increasingly isolated position, which could give the impression of offering itself to the illegal trade in cultural goods. Of course, we do not want to give this impression, ladies and gentlemen, of course. We will also not find it difficult to fulfil the commitments made in respect of cultural goods return. Mr Ostermayer has already pointed out that we already have many relevant rules. Yes, there is already, for example, at EU level a directive that relates to the return of cultural goods unlawfully transferred from the territory of a Member State. This Directive has also been implemented in Austria by allowing for the judicial enforcement of claims for return in the extrajudicial proceedings. Now, the current implementing law is being replaced by a new federal law, which includes both the implementation of the directives and the implementation of the Convention. In particular, the existing uncertainties in dealing with the recovery of cultural goods from countries outside the euro area will also be addressed. European Union. As has already been pointed out by my previous speakers, there is nothing against the ratification of this Convention. Thank you.

This is actually the case with the least of the nursing children. This amendment is about giving those who do not have a common residence the opportunity to take this baby-month away from this adoption plan. Against this background: It was a good first step, but let's move on quickly so that all parents in Austria can take this baby-month and not just those who can afford it, and not just those who are in the public service.

I am pleased that I am also allowed to report here in the House that we are also fulfilling and implementing this task with this paper. The Service Law Novelle, which is negotiated in social partnership, preserves the income of public servants. How does that happen? – This is done by means of a so-called maintenance allowance. At the next biennial jump, the salary is kept on the amount which would be granted according to the old system. ==References====External links== January 2015 decided to set up a system of pre-emption, and the staff are in the so-called plus phase. Structurally, this model does not create any additional costs for the federal government – because it was also a call from this resolution that structurally no additional costs should be incurred. This is due to a preferential effect of one-off additional costs, but these are also rebalanced by the entire system. Why do I say that now? – Of course, it must not be forgotten why we were not forced to do this step, but had to put it: because we once had a budget risk of €3.5 billion and structural additional costs of € 700 million. From this point of view, I would like to thank the Public Service Union, which was seeking a solution, for the constructive and intensive negotiations and also for the trade union of postal and telecommunications workers, with which we have also had very, very constructive discussions. For the explanation only: The judges' lawyers are not being transferred through the new system because they receive a fixed content, but we have been able to reach a compromise in so far as all times beyond the legal year, which currently lasts 5 months, are also counted. But, ladies and gentlemen, we have not only dealt with the maintenance allowance at this Novelle service law, but we also have other aspects of a change in the law of the service here, such as the baby month in question, because the opening of the paternal month for same-sex couples and adoption and thus the transformation to the baby month was and is a special concern to me. Therefore, I am pleased that we can implement this baby month in this Novelle Service Law and in this regard the public service plays a pioneering role in the field of equal treatment in addition to many other things. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] Another step that we take with this Service Law Novelle is the introduction of real military-time careers, because after the six-month basic defence service, those who seek a career receive no extension of the presence service, but a real temporary public service relationship, and this also includes the fact that the full-time periods of service are considered to be for a later federal service. The previous foreign presences will also be completed in a service relationship in the future, and this is further progress. I would like to briefly address two requests which have already been made to Mr Ostermayer in the Constitutional Committee, namely the question of warning and the question of full capacity for action. I have, of course, taken these requests very, very seriously and tried to implement them with the Public Service Trade Union. In one case I succeeded, unfortunately not yet. The Novelle Service Law contains a provision to annihilate warnings and instructions after three years if the official has no longer committed any further breach of service. As regards the elimination of discrimination against applicants with limited capacity for action, I have already announced to the Public Service Trade Union, as the Federal Minister Ostermayer has already announced in the Committee, that it should no longer be applied to the full capacity of action, but only to the necessary or planned capacity to act. This would also enable persons with limited capacity to conclude a regular service relationship. Unfortunately, it has not yet been possible to reach agreement with the Public Service Trade Union in this respect, but I will of course continue to pursue this issue with the next Novelle Service Law – as you know – every autumn. In the meantime – and I would like to stress this here too – it is possible that such applicants can also be recruited through a special contract. For example, special contracts also receive pilots or doctors – just so that one can mention the completeness in half. Ladies and gentlemen, the Novelle Service Law contains, as has been said, in addition to the maintenance allowance and the fulfilment of the motion for a resolution, substantial changes in service law, and I therefore ask them to have a broad consent. - Thank you.

This would have the great advantage that teachers are also automatically longer in school. We also want to enable a significantly more flexible use of teachers in schools. At present, it is hardly possible to make a rapid change from one week to the next. However, this is very often required in schools – ‘project education’, ‘Vienna week’, ‘ski lessons’ or more. I think we would be well advised to allow this flexible use of teachers in schools. Such a model would also have the advantage of taking particular loads into account for school-specific purposes. I am thinking, for example, of test-intensive times in which not all teachers are equally burdened. I also think that we would create opportunities for teachers to rise, e.g. team coordination, someone who coordinates the languages at a school, someone who coordinates the sub-level, who is a specialist board, whatever. All these possibilities do not exist at present. At present, we need to take advantage of the possibilities set out by the Confederation. For example, a German hour is worth 1,167, regardless of whether in the class 10 students sit inside or 30 or more. This is not fair, and it is not considered fair in schools either. We therefore need clear action in this regard. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] The model decided at the time has another disadvantage: It is a disadvantage to those teachers whom we should particularly support, such as the teachers at public schools, who are significantly disadvantaged by the model decided at the time and who deserve less than their colleagues in secondary and higher schools. We need to discuss this kind of thing, so we'll pick up the big solution instead of putting it back on the old model! My request to the ÖVP: Please fulfill to former President Fritz Neugebauer the wish he expressed in his last speech! Thank you.
